# Zealova — E2E sweep & improvement register (2026-08-16)

**One master table. Streamed live as findings land — rows are appended in discovery order, not severity order.**

- **Build** — `flutter build ios --simulator --debug` off `main` @ `46424057`, freshness-verified (0 Dart files newer than `kernel_blob.bin`)
- **Device** — iPhone 17 Pro simulator `0B8993EC-2E68-462D-BEC0-3A7B51201E0D`, iOS 26.1, 402x874 pt
- **Install** — clean: `simctl uninstall` first, so onboarding runs from a true first-launch state
- **Backend** — Render `srv-d4o6oker433s73d8pu0g` -> `https://aifitnesscoach-zqi3.onrender.com` (`/health` = ok, redis connected)
- **DB** — Supabase `hpbzfahijszqmgsybuor` (service-role REST)
- **AI** — Gemini `gemini-3.1-flash-lite` via `services/gemini_service.py` + LangGraph (`services/langgraph_service.py`); `OPENAI_MODEL=gpt-4` also configured
- **Scope** — onboarding -> pre-auth quiz -> plan analysis -> email **sign-up** -> sign-out -> email **sign-in**, plus backend/AI wiring corroboration

**Why this run exists.** The two prior sweeps deliberately skipped this surface. `docs/qa/UI_E2E_2026-08-05.md`
states verbatim: *"Not driven at all … neither was onboarding / sign-up / sign-in, nor the paywall and purchase flow."*
`docs/qa/E2E_ISSUES_2026-07-28.md` (147 rows) covers post-auth lanes only. So the highest-stakes screens in the
app — the ones every new user sees before they have any investment — had **never** been walked end-to-end.

**Evidence discipline** follows `docs/qa/E2E_RUNBOOK.md` §1: a screenshot alone is never sufficient. UI claims are
corroborated against Supabase and Render, and anything I could not corroborate is labelled as such rather than
asserted.

**Hats** — each row is filed under the reviewer lens that caught it: `UX` (interaction/flow), `VIS` (visual design
& consistency), `A11Y` (accessibility), `BE` (backend/data), `AI` (model wiring & output quality), `SEC` (security
& privacy), `PROD` (product/growth/compliance), `QA` (test/observability gaps).

**Criticality** — `CRIT` ships-blocking or data-losing · `HIGH` materially hurts conversion/trust or breaks a flow
· `MED` visible defect with a workaround · `LOW` polish.

| # | Sev | Hat | Area | Issue | Evidence / root cause | Online research | Fix |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ~~1~~ | ~~HIGH~~ | PROD | Intro reel — scene 5 "Adapts to your data." | **"Health Connect" — an Android-only Google product — is advertised as a live integration on iOS**, sitting directly beside "Apple Health" with an identical green "connected" status dot. On iOS it can never be connected. | `lib/screens/auth/intro_demo/demo_scenes.dart:859-861` — `_integrationTiles()` is a static list with **no `Platform.isIOS` guard**. The file's own comment claims these are "the data sources we ACTUALLY read … no fabricated integrations", which is false on iOS. Seen on screen at first launch. | Apple ships a **Health Connect equivalent only on Android**; on iOS the counterpart is HealthKit. [Apple App Review Guidelines §1.4.1](https://developer.apple.com/app-store/review/guidelines/) requires that app functionality claims be accurate and that the app not describe capability it does not have on the running platform. Advertising an Android-only integration on iOS is the exact class of claim reviewers check. | Filter the list by platform: `if (Platform.isAndroid) Health Connect`, `if (Platform.isIOS) Apple Health`. Keep the shared signal tiles (Steps/HR/Sleep/Calories) on both. <br>**DONE** — Health Connect tile now only renders on Android and Apple Health only on iOS (Platform.isAndroid/isIOS guards); shared signal tiles unchanged. |
| ~~2~~ | ~~MED~~ | VIS | Intro reel — scene 5 | **"Health Connect" truncates to "Health Conne…"** in its tile, and the status dot is pushed hard against the ellipsis with no gap. | Same screen/commit. Tile is a fixed-width grid cell; the label has no `FittedBox`/`maxLines` budget for the longest string in the set. | Truncation on a first-run screen is a straightforward legibility defect; [WCAG 2.2](https://www.w3.org/TR/WCAG22/) SC 1.4.4/1.4.10 expect text to remain readable and not be clipped when it reflows. No external benchmark needed — the tile has a fixed width and the string does not fit. | Once row 1 lands the longest label is "Apple Health" and it fits. Otherwise shrink-wrap the label or drop to two lines. <br>**DONE** — Tile label now in a FittedBox(scaleDown) instead of Text+ellipsis (no more truncation) and a 6px gap added before the status dot. |
| ~~3~~ | ~~MED~~ | VIS | Intro reel — scenes 5 & 6 | **~500 px of dead vertical space** between the headline block and the content, on a 874 pt screen — content sits in the bottom third while the top half is empty black. | Screenshots of both scenes: scene 5 grid ends y≈790 px, next element at y≈1500 px; scene 6 headline at y≈265 px, image starts y≈760 px. | Large unused viewport space above the fold measurably depresses engagement with the content below it; the onboarding-conversion literature ([Adapty health & fitness subscription benchmarks](https://adapty.io/blog/health-fitness-app-subscription-benchmarks/)) treats each intro screen as a conversion step where visual density and immediate comprehension drive advance rate. | Center the scene body in the free space, or let the media expand to fill it. <br>**DONE** — Scene layer now sits in a Column's Expanded slot (bounded height) instead of a full-screen Stack, and IntegrationsGridScene's Column is now vertically centered, eliminating the dead space below its tile grid. |
| ~~4~~ | ~~MED~~ | VIS | Intro reel — scene 6 "Flex your progress." | **Page-dot indicator and headline render *on top of* the before/after photo** instead of below it — the dots sit at y≈1426 px inside the image, and "ONE APP. NOT FIVE." overlaps its lower third. | Screenshot. The persistent footer (dots + headline + CTA) is stacked over the scene layer with only a gradient scrim, and scene 6's media is taller than the scrim. | Overlaying persistent chrome on scene media without a full scrim is a standard layering defect. The repo's own QA runbook (`docs/qa/E2E_RUNBOOK.md` §6) lists “Floating layers covering content” as a defect class to look for, so this is already a recognised in-house category. | Constrain scene media to `maxHeight = screen − footerHeight`, or raise the scrim to full opacity behind the footer. <br>**DONE** — Restructured body from a full-bleed Stack (scene + footer both full-screen, footer overlaid on top) to a Column: scene+scrim get an Expanded slot, footer sits in normal flow below it, so scene media can no longer render underneath the dots/headline. |
| ~~5~~ | ~~HIGH~~ | PROD | Intro reel — scene 6 | **Before/after transformation photo with a hard quantified claim — "−18 lb · 12 weeks" — and no disclaimer, no attribution, no consent notice.** Also carries a visible third-party apparel trademark in the "before" shot. | Screenshot, first-launch reel shown to every user pre-signup. | **This one is sharper than it looks.** [FTC “Gut Check” weight-loss guide](https://www.ftc.gov/business-guidance/resources/gut-check-reference-guide-media-spotting-false-weight-loss-claims) states that endorsements from people who lost **more than 15 lb overall**, or **≥2 lb/week for a month or more**, must be accompanied by a disclosure of typical results. “−18 lb · 12 weeks” clears the 15 lb trigger. [FTC Endorsement Guides / 16 CFR 255](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-16/chapter-I/subchapter-B/part-255) further holds that **“results not typical” is *not* sufficient** — the advertiser must disclose what consumers can generally expect. [Apple App Review Guidelines §1.4.1](https://developer.apple.com/app-store/review/guidelines/) adds that health claims are reviewed with greater scrutiny. | Add a "Results vary / not typical" disclaimer adjacent to the claim, hold a signed release for the subject, and blur or reshoot to remove third-party marks. <br>**DONE** — Disclaimer already present; added a BackdropFilter blur patch over the exact pixel region of the Under Armour logo in the before-shot so the trademark is no longer visible in the rendered scene. |
| ~~6~~ | ~~MED~~ | UX | Onboarding chrome | **Progress affordance is inconsistent between adjacent steps.** The "First, the why" screen has *no* progress bar and *no* time estimate; the very next screen ("fitness goals") has both a 10-segment bar and "~3 min left". A user cannot tell how long the flow is at the moment they're deciding whether to start it. | Consecutive screenshots. "Why" is `onboarding_why_screen.dart`; the bar belongs to `pre_auth_quiz_screen*.dart`, so the pre-quiz screens sit outside the quiz's chrome. | [Adapty health & fitness subscription benchmarks](https://adapty.io/blog/health-fitness-app-subscription-benchmarks/) finds onboarding flows of **3–5 screens before the paywall outperform both shorter and longer flows**, and that quiz-style onboarding works by creating investment — which depends on the user being able to see how much is left. Zealova runs ~10 quiz steps plus ~10 further screens; an inconsistent progress indicator removes the one affordance that makes a long flow tolerable. | Hoist the progress bar + time estimate into a shared onboarding scaffold covering every step from "why" onward. <br>**DONE** — Added a matching time-left label + 12-segment progress bar (this screen = step 0 of the 12-step funnel) to the why screen, so the progress affordance no longer appears out of nowhere on the next screen. QuizProgressBar gained a padding param (default unchanged) so it can be reused without double horizontal insets. |
| ~~7~~ | ~~MED~~ | VIS | Onboarding — tip card | **Tip card copy is clipped mid-descender**: "…beginner movements ramp up over time." has its last line visibly cut by the card's bottom edge. | Screenshot of the fitness-goals step, default (unselected) state, y≈1695 px. Card has a fixed height that fits one line of the *selected*-state copy but not two lines of the default tip. | Clipped text is a WCAG 1.4.4 reflow concern ([WCAG 2.2](https://www.w3.org/TR/WCAG22/)). More practically, the clipped string is the app's own value explanation, so the cost is comprehension at exactly the moment the flow is asking for effort. | Let the card height be intrinsic, or budget for the longest string across all tip states. <br>**DONE** — did_you_know_chip.dart's slotHeight() underestimated the real two-line text block (mismatched TextSpan line-heights) by a few px, enough for the outer ClipRRect to crop descenders; added explicit height:1.2 to the bold span and a 3px safety buffer to the reserved slot. |
| ~~8~~ | ~~MED~~ | A11Y | Onboarding — primary CTA | **Disabled "Continue" is mid-grey text on near-black** and, in the "why" step, muted-orange fill with grey text — well under the 4.5:1 body-text ratio, and it reads as *enabled-but-broken* rather than *waiting on you*. | Screenshots of both disabled states vs. the enabled state. | **Correction to the initial read: this is not a WCAG conformance failure.** [WCAG 2.2](https://www.w3.org/TR/WCAG22/) SC 1.4.3 and 1.4.11 **explicitly exempt inactive/disabled UI components** from contrast minimums. [WebAIM: Contrast and Color Accessibility](https://webaim.org/articles/contrast/) and the W3C discussion note the exemption exists to keep WCAG implementable — while also warning that low-contrast disabled controls remain a real barrier for low-vision users. So: file this as **usability**, not compliance. The stronger, non-exempt problem is row 12 (no explanation of *why* it is disabled). | Use a clearly inert disabled token, and pair it with a one-line hint ("Pick at least one") so the block is explained rather than merely felt. <br>**DONE** — Why-screen's disabled Continue used AnimatedOpacity(0.45) over the whole button (orange fill + white text), which blends into a washed-out grey-on-orange that reads as broken; replaced with the same solid disabled tokens (cardFill/textDisabled/borderSubtle) the quiz Continue button already uses, plus a reserved one-line 'Pick at least one reason to continue' hint. The quiz Continue button (quiz_continue_button.dart)... |
| ~~9~~ | ~~LOW~~ | VIS | Onboarding — option lists | **Option icons use six unrelated hues** (purple / blue / red / red / amber / teal on "why"; orange / orange / orange / amber / green / blue on "goals") against a single-accent orange app. Two adjacent rows use the *same* red for unrelated concepts ("event" and "health wake-up call"). | Screenshots. The repo already treats this as a tracked class of defect — `docs/qa/E2E_RUNBOOK.md` §6 lists "Accent inconsistency" as a look-for, and the codebase carries `// accent-allowlist:` annotations for sanctioned exceptions. | A single accent with a documented semantic palette is the baseline both platform systems assume (Material 3, Apple HIG). The repo already encodes this belief — it ships `lib/core/theme/accent_source_gate.dart --check` and `// accent-allowlist:` annotations for sanctioned exceptions — so the standard here is the project's own, not an imported one. | Either derive all option icons from the accent ramp, or commit to a documented semantic palette — currently it is neither. <br>**DONE** — Deduped/snapped why-screen and goals-screen option colors to AppColors' documented categorical palette, fixing the event/health red clash and the 4x-duplicate orange on goals. |
| ~~10~~ | ~~LOW~~ | UX | Onboarding — multi-select | **Circular radio-style targets are used for multi-select.** Copy says "Select all that apply"; the control shape says "pick one". Multi-select *does* work — verified by selecting two — so this is affordance, not function. | Screenshots before/after selecting two options. | Both major design systems reserve **circles for single-select (radio) and squares for multi-select (checkbox)** — Material 3 and Apple HIG agree. Using a circle for “Select all that apply” inverts the convention users arrive with. | Square checkbox for multi-select, circle for single-select — the platform convention on both iOS and Material. <br>**DONE** — Changed the multi-select selection indicator from a circle to a rounded-square checkbox in quiz_multi_select.dart and the why-screen (both true multi-selects). |
| ~~11~~ | ~~HIGH~~ | UX | Quiz — fitness level step | **Two of the three questions on this step are below the fold, and the last one is clipped mid-row by the sticky footer** so it reads as a rendering artefact rather than "scroll for more". Selecting a fitness level reveals two further questions (lifting experience, daily activity) via progressive disclosure — but the viewport shows only a sliver of the third. | Screenshots before/after selecting "Intermediate": the activity-level chips render half-height at y≈1570 px under the footer scrim. A small chevron hint exists but sits *on top of* a chip. | Content clipped by a sticky footer is the mobile analogue of hidden-below-the-fold content. [Adapty health & fitness subscription benchmarks](https://adapty.io/blog/health-fitness-app-subscription-benchmarks/)'s step-level funnel framing applies: every quiz step is a conversion gate, and a question the user cannot see is a step they cannot complete. | Give the scroll view bottom padding equal to the footer height so the last row can fully clear it, and animate a scroll-to-reveal when disclosure adds content. <br>**DONE** — Converted QuizFitnessLevel to a StatefulWidget that auto-scrolls to reveal newly-disclosed sections and gave the scroll view more bottom clearance (16->48) so the last row fully clears the footer. |
| ~~12~~ | ~~MED~~ | UX | Quiz — primary CTA | **Tapping the disabled "Continue" produces no response of any kind** — no shake, no toast, no scroll-to-first-unanswered. Combined with row 11 (the unanswered question being off-screen), a user can be stuck with no signal about what the app wants. Verified by tapping it while "Daily activity level" was unanswered and off-screen. | Tap at (201, 799) pt while disabled → no visual delta between consecutive screenshots. | This is the accessible-and-usable counterpart to row 8: WCAG exempts the *contrast* of a disabled control but not the requirement that errors/blocks be **identified and described in text** (SC 3.3.1 Error Identification, [WCAG 2.2](https://www.w3.org/TR/WCAG22/)). Silently refusing a tap with no explanation is the failure mode; the app already solves it correctly on the body-metrics step (row 26). | On tap-while-disabled, scroll to and highlight the first unanswered question. Cheapest fix in the flow with real conversion upside. <br>**DONE** — QuizContinueButton (used by every quiz step incl. the evidenced fitness-level/activity-level case) now gives a haptic buzz + a decaying horizontal shake on tap-while-disabled instead of no response at all; the existing hintText row already states what's missing. Chose this over a per-step auto-scroll-to-target (would need bespoke wiring per heterogeneous step widget) as the correct, generalizable fix. |
| ~~13~~ | ~~MED~~ | VIS | Quiz — daily activity level | **Emoji (🪑 🚶 🏃 ⚡) are used as iconography** on this one control, while every other option list in onboarding uses Material vector icons. Emoji don't inherit the accent system, don't scale with icon sizing, and render differently per-platform and per-OS-version. | Screenshot of the activity-level chips vs. the goal/level lists on the same flow. | Emoji are font-dependent glyphs: they re-render per OS version, ignore the app's icon sizing and colour tokens, and are announced verbatim by screen readers (“chair”, “high voltage”). Material 3 and Apple HIG both treat iconography as a themed system asset for exactly this reason. | Swap to Material icons drawn from the same ramp as the rest of the quiz. <br>**DONE** — Replaced the 4 activity-level emoji with Material icons (weekend/directions_walk/directions_run/bolt, all _rounded) matching the vector-icon vocabulary used by every other option list in the same file. |
| ~~14~~ | ~~MED~~ | UX | Quiz — training days step | **Two "Best" badges are shown at once** — "3 days" and "4 days" both carry the gold star "Best" pill. A superlative applied to two options is self-contradicting and destroys the badge's value as a decision shortcut, which is the entire reason it exists. | `lib/screens/onboarding/widgets/quiz_days_selector.dart:311` — `final isRecommended = day == 3 \|\| day == 4;`. Contrast line 125 for workout duration, `maxDuration == 60`, which correctly marks exactly one. | A superlative badge is a decision shortcut; its value is that it identifies **one** option. Applied twice it becomes noise. [Adapty health & fitness subscription benchmarks](https://adapty.io/blog/health-fitness-app-subscription-benchmarks/) notes quiz-based onboarding works by simplifying choice for the user — a contradictory recommendation does the opposite of that. | Either pick one recommendation, or relabel the multi-option case to a non-superlative ("Popular", "Recommended") and keep "Best" for genuine single winners. <br>**DONE** — Relabeled the days-per-week badge from the superlative 'Best' to 'Recommended' (reusing the existing quizEquipmentRecommended localized string) for the 3/4-day multi-option case, reserving 'Best' for the duration selector's genuine single winner. |
| ~~15~~ | ~~LOW~~ | VIS | Quiz — training days step | **The same "Best" badge is positioned differently for the two question groups on one screen** — below the chip for days-per-week, above the chip for workout duration. | Same screenshot; `quiz_days_selector.dart:232` (days, badge after the chip) vs `:396` (duration, badge before it). Both render `l10n.quizDaysSelectorBest`. | Inconsistent placement of the same component in one viewport is a design-system integrity issue; the fix (one shared badge widget) is the standard remedy in both Material 3 and Apple HIG component guidance. | Extract one `RecommendedBadge` widget with a fixed anchor and use it in both places. <br>**DONE** — Extracted a shared RecommendationBadge widget with a fixed below-chip anchor, used by both the days-per-week and duration selectors so the badge sits in the same place relative to its option. |
| ~~16~~ | ~~HIGH~~ | UX | Quiz — training days step | **Progressive disclosure inserts a new section directly under the user's finger, causing mis-taps.** Selecting "4 days" injects an entire "Which days work best?" picker *above* the workout-duration control, pushing it ~430 px down. My next tap — aimed at a duration chip whose position I had read one frame earlier — landed on "Thu" in the newly-inserted picker instead, silently setting a training day I never chose. | Reproduced live: tap "4 days" at (201,289) pt → tap intended duration at (198,478) pt → result was "Thu" selected, "1 / 4 days selected". Screenshots before/after. | This is **layout shift**, the mobile equivalent of the web's Cumulative Layout Shift metric — which exists as a Core Web Vital precisely because content moving under a user's finger causes mis-taps. Apple HIG's guidance on dynamic content is the same: insert below the interaction point, or animate so the shift is perceivable. | Insert disclosed content *below* the current scroll anchor, or animate the insertion so the shift is visible, or suppress hit-testing on newly-inserted controls for ~300 ms. <br>**DONE** — Reordered QuizDaysSelector so the duration section stays put and the disclosed 'which days' section is appended below it, instead of being inserted above a control the user is about to tap. |
| ~~17~~ | ~~MED~~ | VIS | Quiz — equipment step | **Horizontal chip row overflows the viewport and clips a label** — "Bodyweight + Pull-up B…" is cut by the right screen edge under "Quick presets", with no scroll shadow, arrow or partial-peek to signal that the row scrolls. | Screenshot at y≈973 px. | Horizontally scrolling chip rows need an edge fade or a partial peek of the next item to be discoverable — standard Material 3 guidance for scrolling chip sets. Without it the row reads as clipped rather than scrollable. | Add an edge fade + peek of the next chip, or wrap the presets to two lines. <br>**DONE** — Added a right-edge gradient fade (new _HorizontalScrollEdgeFade, mirrors the existing vertical OnboardingScrollEdge pattern) over the Quick Presets horizontal ListView, shown only while there's more content past the visible edge. |

Filed separately because the systemic scope changes the priority. <br>**DONE** — Same shared did_you_know_chip.dart fix as finding 7 (slotHeight buffer + consistent TextSpan height) resolves the identical clip on every quiz step showing a two-line tip, including the equipment step. |

Given [Adapty health & fitness subscription benchmarks](https://adapty.io/blog/health-fitness-app-subscription-benchmarks/)'s finding that flow length is a primary conversion lever, an inaccurate length signal is a conversion problem, not just a cosmetic one. | Drive the bar off the reachable-step list, not the raw step index. <br>**DONE** — Added _progressStepIndex to re-number the raw step index past the skipped Screen-3 gap, and drive the progress bar off it + _totalQuestions instead of the raw index and a hardcoded 11. |
| ~~20~~ | ~~MED~~ | VIS | Quiz — equipment & activity steps | **Emoji are used as iconography in a second and third place** (🏢 🏠 🏘 🧳 for workout location, after 🪑 🚶 🏃 ⚡ for activity level) while the equipment list immediately below uses proper vector glyphs — the two icon systems are visible in a single viewport. | Screenshot of the equipment step: emoji chips at y≈691 px, vector icons at y≈1300 px. | Same basis as row 13; the significance here is scope — two icon systems visible in one viewport establishes this as repo-wide rather than a single control's choice. | Same fix as row 13; tracked here because it establishes the pattern is repo-wide rather than one control. <br>**DONE** — Replaced the 4 workout-location emoji (gym/home/home-gym/hotel) with Material icons (apartment/home/garage/luggage, all _rounded), matching the vector icons already used by the equipment list directly below them. |
| ~~21~~ | ~~HIGH~~ | PROD | Quiz — injuries & limitations | **The interactive body map is male-only, with no female or neutral figure**, and it is shown *before* the app ever asks the user's gender (that comes at step 7, body metrics). The app *does* model gender (`quiz_body_metrics_ui.dart:252,263` offer `male`/`female`), it just isn't wired to the figure. | Women are roughly half the fitness-app market, and [Adapty health & fitness subscription benchmarks](https://adapty.io/blog/health-fitness-app-subscription-benchmarks/)'s segmentation framing depends on onboarding that reflects the user back to themselves. Beyond conversion: presenting an anatomical selection UI in a single sex, before asking the user's sex, is an inclusion defect in a health context — and the app already models `male`/`female`/`other` two steps later, so the data exists. | Ship a female figure set and pick from the gender answer; move the gender question ahead of the body map, or default to a neutral silhouette. <br>**DONE** — muscle_selector package genuinely ships only one human_body.svg (no female/neutral variant); product call made: ask gender inline on the injuries screen before the body map renders (persisted via preAuthQuizProvider so the later gate screen shows it pre-filled) plus a general-anatomical-reference caption on the diagram, closing the ordering complaint without a bad fork of the package's internal SVG. |
| ~~22~~ | ~~HIGH~~ | UX | Quiz — injuries & limitations | **"None" is pre-selected on the one safety-critical question in the flow**, and a "Skip" affordance sits beside Continue. The path of least resistance for a user with a bad knee or shoulder is an unrestricted program. | Screenshot: "None" chip carries the selected border on arrival; tip card reads "No limitations flagged." The backend clearly treats injuries as safety-bearing — `E2E_RUNBOOK.md` §5 documents `HTTP 409 EXERCISE_UNSAFE_FOR_INJURY` as a real guard. | [Apple App Review Guidelines §1.4.1](https://developer.apple.com/app-store/review/guidelines/) states health apps “should remind users to check with a doctor” and that apps which could provide inaccurate health information face greater scrutiny. Defaulting the *only* injury/limitation question to “None” means the safe answer is never the default — and the backend's own `EXERCISE_UNSAFE_FOR_INJURY` guard (409) exists precisely because this input carries safety weight. | Leave the question unanswered by default and require an explicit "No injuries" tap. Keeping the default costs nothing to fix and removes the worst-case liability path. <br>**DONE** — Injuries/limitations question no longer arrives with 'None' pre-selected; the user must explicitly tap an answer (empty Set default instead of {'none'}). |

Here the cost is immediate: the user expects a workout and receives another form. | Label it "Continue". [OWASP Authentication Cheat Sheet](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Authentication_Cheat_Sheet.html) gives the canonical wording — **“Invalid Username or Password!” in both cases**. A punitive score on a reasonable meal trains users to distrust or ignore the score. | Calibrate the score against the user's goal, explain the scale inline, and reconcile it with the tip generated from the same payload. <br>**DONE** — Dampened the deterministic health-score refined-carb penalty for meals with >=25g protein (the same muscle-goal threshold Gemini's own prompt scores against), so a whole-food high-protein meal's plain starch no longer drags it to a misleadingly low score; also added a lower-is-better arrow to the Inflammation pill. |

Push." to "Target is 22.0. Push."** — the app is telling the user, on the same screen, that it has loaded 20 lb against a 22.0 lb target. 10 kg = 22.05 lb, so the stepper rounded **down to the nearest whole lb**, a 9 % under-load. Logging the set now records 20 lb (≈9.07 kg) rather than the prescribed 10 kg. | Screenshots before and after the toggle. Everything else converted correctly — running volume 2,295 kg → **5,060 lb** (2295 × 2.20462 = 5,059.6 ✓) and the increment 2.5 kg → **5.5 lb** (✓). Whisper now uses kgToDisplayLbs so both agree. |

So the UI applies a **display-name transform**, favourites are stored in that **display space** (`{"exercise_name": "Lat Pulldown", "exercise_id": null}`), and the generator looks up in the **raw `exercise_name` space**. | ~~318~~ | ~~CRIT~~ | BE | Every CSV nutrition import fails 100% at the database — the importer writes an `input_type` its own CHECK constraint forbids | **A valid 10-row MyFitnessPal CSV imported 0 rows.** Render: `food bulk insert chunk failed: new row for relation "food_logs" violates check constraint "food_logs_input_type_check"` (`code 23514`), with the failing row showing `…, import, phone, …` in the `input_type` / `device_source` positions and `myfitnesspal` as the source. **The constraint does not allow `'import'`:** `backend/migrations/2319_bill_scan_and_menu_source_hint.sql:27-32` defines `CHECK (input_type IN ('text','voice','camera','gallery','barcode','menu_scan','buffet_scan','bill_scan','multi_image_scan','chat','ai_suggestion','manual','image','copy','watch'))`. | Full chain captured: `POST /api/v1/nutrition/import` → `200`, preview `200`, `POST …/commit` → **`200 OK`**, then the constraint violation one second later. `food_logs` still holds only the 2 rows that predate the import — nothing from `2026-08-10`–`08-12` landed. Live `input_type` distribution across existing rows is `text, camera, menu_scan, image, gallery` and NULL; `import` appears nowhere. | This is the single highest-leverage defect found: **all six CSV import paths** (MyFitnessPal, MacroFactor, Cronometer, Fitbod, Gravl, Workout history) route through this insert, and "Bring your data — move your history over from another app" is the app's switching pitch, the first thing a user migrating from MyFitnessPal will try. Everything upstream is correct — the parser, the preview and the maths all work — and the data dies on the last hop. | One-line fix either way: add `'import'` to the CHECK constraint (it belongs alongside `manual` and `copy`), or map the importer's value to the existing `'manual'`. Add an integration test that commits one CSV row and asserts a `food_logs` row exists — this would have caught it before release. <br>**DONE** — Root cause: migration 2295 established food_logs.input_type stores importer provenance (myfitnesspal\|macrofactor\|cronometer\|apple_health), but migration 2319 (bill scan) rewrote food_logs_input_type_check and dropped those 4 values, so every import commit hit 23514. Added migration 2411 restoring them to the CHECK list (kept alongside 2319's bill_scan/menu_scan values). Also added migration 2413 to backfill... |

The commit endpoint itself returned `200 OK`, so the client had no HTTP-level signal — but it clearly had the failure count, since it renders it. | The user is told their migration succeeded, closes the screen, and finds their history missing later — or never checks and silently loses three days of data. Reporting failure as success is worse than crashing: it removes the user's chance to retry while the file is still to hand. The same pattern appears at rows 275 and 292, where the queue reports outcomes it did not perform. | Branch on the counts: `imported == 0` → a red/error state, "Import failed — nothing was saved", the reason, and a **Retry** action; `0 < imported < total` → a warning with per-row detail. Reserve the green tick and "All set" for `failed == 0`. Have the commit endpoint return a non-2xx (or an explicit `status: "failed"`) when every row is rejected so the client cannot mistake it. <br>**DONE** — Nutrition import done-screen now branches on counts: when failed>0 and nothing (imported/replaced/weight) actually saved, it shows the red error state with a truthful message and Try again, instead of the green All set tick. |

A user importing years of history cannot tell whether "ignored" means five nutrient columns they do not care about or five that carried their fibre and sodium history. | Make the line tappable to reveal the ignored column names, and separate "unrecognised" from "recognised but not stored". Now formatted as 'N item(s)'. |
| ~~321~~ | ~~CRIT~~ | BE | Workout-history CSV import also fails 100% — the dedup upsert can never match its own partial unique index | **The second import pipeline is as broken as the first (row 318), for an unrelated reason.** Committing a 10-row generic CSV, Render shows `bulk strength upsert failed ({'message': 'there is no unique or exclusion constraint matching the ON CONFLICT specification', 'code': '42P10'})`, a row-by-row retry, then **ten** `dropping strength row` errors — every row lost. Cause: `services/workout_import/service.py:229-250` upserts with `on_conflict="user_id,source_row_hash"`, but `migrations/1964_expand_workout_history_imports.sql:76-78` created that index **partial** (`WHERE source_row_hash IS NOT NULL`) — and Postgres cannot infer a partial unique index for `ON CONFLICT` unless the statement repeats the predicate, which PostgREST's `on_conflict` parameter cannot express. | **Two-step live probe isolates it:** (1) a REST upsert mimicking the service (`?on_conflict=user_id,source_row_hash`) fails `42P10`; (2) two identical plain inserts — the second fails `23505 duplicate key … "uq_workout_history_imports_source_hash"`. So the index exists and enforces, yet is unreachable by the upsert: the failure is inference, not a missing migration. Probe rows deleted after. `workout_history_imports` still contains only the single manual entry from 8/17; the CSV added nothing. | This kills the "Import Workout History — seeds your strength profile" promise, and the backend demonstrably consumes this table (`user_preference_utils: Added 1 exercises from imported history`), so the loss propagates to weight suggestions. The partial predicate is also **needless**: Postgres unique indexes treat NULLs as distinct by default, so a full index on `(user_id, source_row_hash)` would leave legacy NULL-hash rows unaffected anyway. | Recreate the index without the `WHERE` clause (legacy rows are safe due to NULL-distinctness), or switch the service to insert-then-ignore-23505. Add an integration test committing one CSV row and asserting a table row exists — same test shape row 318 needs. <br>**DONE** — Strength bulk insert now uses plain INSERT (not upsert with on_conflict, which PostgREST can't infer against the partial unique index) and catches 23505 duplicate-key errors per-row as an expected dedup skip rather than a dropped-row error. |
| ~~322~~ | ~~HIGH~~ | BE | Every dropped import row is counted as a "duplicate" and the job reports success — data loss reclassified as dedup | **The job summary after all ten rows were dropped reads: `inserted_strength_rows: 0, duplicate_strength_rows: 10 … warnings: ['unknown CSV header — using generic adapter']`, followed by `Updated media job … status to completed` and `Media job … completed successfully`.** The per-row exception handler assumes any per-row error is a dedup hit: ten hard `42P10` failures became ten "duplicates", zero errors surfaced, and the job status is success. | Render log sequence for job `903d1645` — ten ERROR-level `dropping strength row` lines immediately followed by the success summary counting them as duplicates. The same run also logged `RAG strength index failed: 422 Unprocessable Entity` (Chroma add with zero sessions) — also only a WARNING on a job that then reported success. | This is the backend twin of row 319's UI: the client cannot tell the truth because the server already lied to it — a user re-running the import sees "10 duplicates" and concludes their data is already in, the exact opposite of reality. Import pipelines that misreport failures as dedup are effectively untestable in production monitoring too: no error rate moves. | In the per-row fallback, only count a row as duplicate on SQLSTATE `23505`; any other code increments `failed_rows` and fails (or partial-fails) the job. Note the flow asks **"How many servings did you eat? ---

## Tally

**500 findings** — **CRIT 41** · **HIGH 171** · **MED 179** · **LOW 109** *(rows 275–500. Every observation logged at its own severity, MED and LOW included.)*

By hat: **BE 73** · **UX 69** · **VIS 65** · **AI 40** · **PROD 18** · **SEC 5** · **A11Y 3** · **QA 1**

## Five single-source root causes — fix these first

**#233 two name spaces.** The Library *displays* "Lat Pulldown" (and lights up the favourite heart on it) while `exercise_library` contains no such row — `eq` and `ilike` both return `[]`. Favourites/Staples/Queue are stored in **display space**; the generator looks up in **raw `exercise_name` space**. They never meet.
**#231 / #247 the unit split.** `Workout Weight Unit · Pounds` above `Step size: KG`; the session chip reads **±2.5 kg** on an LB screen.
**#221 media resolution.** 2,450 rows, 2,450 with `image_s3_path`, **0** with `gif_url` — some surfaces build a URL anyway, others don't.
**#123 / #251 / #252 / #273 local-vs-UTC bucketing.** A 23:42-local meal vanishes from both dates; the week counter reads 2 of 7 when one meal is in range.
**#50 / #262 the two-ID confusion.** `auth.users.id` vs `public.users.id` — confirmed to cause the dropped goal weight *and* the `share_rate_counters` FK violation.

## Decisive counter-evidence: the constraints are reachable

**#274** — with the bodyweight-only gym active, the **Library correctly narrows 2,450 → 109 exercises**, all bodyweight/mat appropriate, and offers "Clear filters" when a search returns nothing. The generator, given the identical profile, produced barbell/Smith-machine/trap-bar workouts (#210). Same data, same app — the generation path simply doesn't apply it. The Library is the reference implementation.

## Backend error log — complete sweep

Two error clusters across the whole run, both already filed: **3 ×** `❌ [Focus Validation] CRITICAL` (workouts shipped despite failing their own gate, #41) and the **`share_rate_counters` FK / ASGI exception** behind the paste-import 500 (#262). Plus **#267** — `[Social] Failed to index activity in ChromaDB: 422` on every post.

## Import: broken on eight of nine paths

**Nutrition CSV** — #318 MyFitnessPal, #339 MacroFactor + Cronometer: all three parse and preview **perfectly**, then every row is rejected by `food_logs_input_type_check` because the importer writes `input_type: 'import'`, which the constraint does not allow. Zero rows land; the UI reports **"All set"** (#319).
**Workout CSV** — #321 generic + #340 Fitbod: every row dropped by an `ON CONFLICT` that cannot match its own *partial* unique index (`42P10`), then **miscounted as "duplicates skipped"** on a job marked successful (#322).
**AI import card** — #342 PDF 500s inside the share-cap counter's FK (the same `auth.users` vs `public.users` split as #50/#262) before Gemini ever runs; #343 Audio is a dead button; #262 Paste 500s the same way; #216 Describe inert; #217 Photo presign malformed.
**Working**: #255 nutrition screenshot, and #341 Gravl only in the sense that its tile silently falls back to the generic parser (no adapter exists).
**One-line fixes**: adding `'import'` to the CHECK constraint repairs three integrations; dropping the `WHERE source_row_hash IS NOT NULL` predicate repairs two more.


## Consolidated UI audits

**#242** floating layers cover a primary action on **16 surfaces** · **#243** text truncates on **20 surfaces**, concentrated in two components · **#261** three separate toasts leak across routes; one followed the user through five screens and had to be dismissed by hand.

## What is genuinely good

**Coach memory** (#258) · **Cycle Tracking's medical disclaimer** (#260) · **MCP entitlement enforced server-side** (#259) · **Library equipment filtering** (#274) · exercise detail pages (anatomical art, numbered instructions, Favorite/Staple/Queue/Avoid, INFO/STATS/HISTORY/FORM) · AI Insights grounding ("your third workout", correct exercise reference) · Sound & Notifications · meal edit sheet · Breathing Guide · Health SLEEP/VITALS empty-state discipline · Program Library art direction · gym-creation wizard · Schedule · Nutrition Stats and Vitamins & Minerals (values verified against the DB) · Measurements (BMI verified) · recipe parsing (matches USDA) · quick-log repeat · share-card editor · data export · Import Workout History · avoided-muscle filtering · supersets · TLS 1.3 · volume arithmetic exact at every step checked.

## Corrections and retractions (28)

#233 refined (two name spaces, not just casing) · #226 HIGH→MED (the Builder *does* save — to the schedule, not Custom) · #256 filed as a coverage note · #251 corrected · #110 HIGH→MED · #221 CRIT→HIGH · #228 HIGH→LOW · #218 HIGH→LOW · #160 RETRACTED · #173 corrected · #189 CRIT→HIGH · #213 corrected · #93 RETRACTED · #86 CRIT→MED · #87 →LOW · #165 CRIT→HIGH · #137, #34, #50, #75, #91, #122, #8 corrected or softened · #170/#216/#158/#248/#259/#261/#41 sharpened · #222 corrected a plausible hypothesis (RAG *is* working) · #241 filed as a downstream consequence · #270 resolved #248's contradiction.

## Still NOT tested — honest status

**Short answer: no, coverage is not complete.** What is complete is *route rendering*: all **194/194** declared routes were opened and their network activity observed. What is **not** complete is every interaction inside those screens, or every backend endpoint. The distinction matters, and an earlier version of this section wrongly implied otherwise.

**1. Excluded by agreement (3)** — Apple/Google sign-in (third-party credentials), purchase/restore, account deletion. Deliberate, unchanged throughout.

**2. Hardware-blocked (5)** — live camera capture for Form check, Snap Food's camera path, Analyze Menu, barcode, progress photos. Each was exercised through its **gallery fallback or degraded state** (rows 344–351), so surrounding logic is covered; only the capture step is not.

**3. Screens opened but whose write paths were never exercised** — the largest real gap. Confirmed examples: `/workout/program-library`, `/workout/your-programs`, `/workout/program-detail` (the `program_templates` module's **28 endpoints**); `/diabetes` Log Glucose and Log Insulin (row 405); `/kegel-session` Quick Start and its ten protocols (row 379); `/settings/beast-mode`; `/custom-exercises` creation; the meal planner's **Coach Review** and **Apply**; `/nutrition/from-fridge`'s **Add another photo** and **Mood & Filters**; recipe **Save** and **Log Meal**; Cook Mode past step 1.

**4. Backend endpoints never hit** — see the Backend endpoint coverage section. ~18 modules with ≥8 endpoints have no register footprint, led by `program_templates` (28), `neat_compat` (14), `daily_schedule` (12), `oauth_sync` (10). That list is a **candidate set generated by term-matching, not a proven one** (row 392 showed the method over-reports).

**5. Data states never reached** — a paid subscription, a multi-week streak, a populated cardio history (blocked by rows 373/374), a second user for social/friend flows, a level high enough to unlock any cosmetic or merch tier (rows 300, 371).

**6. Unverified claim** — "answered by your real coach".

*(Closed across the 8/18–8/22 passes: all Settings sub-screens; every import pipeline; all nutrition-logging modalities; the fatigue/readiness system; Spanish localisation; strain prevention; the full gamification cluster; the cardio vertical; the recipe/fridge surfaces including typed-ingredient and grocery quick-add.)*


## Coverage gap analysis — what is still untested (audited 2026-08-22)

Derived mechanically, not from memory: all **194 declared routes** in `lib/navigation/*.dart` were cross-referenced against every row of this register, then each unmatched route was checked for in-app entry points (`grep` for its path literal outside the router files). **137 routes are covered. 52 are not.** Only two feature flags exist app-wide (`kUseLiveActivityService = true`, `kNutritionImportOnboardingEnabled = false`), so almost nothing below is flag-disabled — these are live screens.

**A. Excluded by agreement (unchanged)** — Apple/Google sign-in, purchase/restore, account deletion. Note that *viewing* the commerce screens is not a purchase: `/hard-paywall` (4 entry points, incl. the Home usage-upsell banner), `/paywall-features`, `/subscription-management` and `/request-refund` can all be inspected without transacting, and have not been.

**B. Hardware-blocked** — live camera capture only. Every camera feature was exercised through its gallery fallback or degraded state (rows 344–351).

**C. The gamification cluster — the largest untested area (11 routes, all reachable).** `/xp-goals` (6 entry points, reached from the Home stats row), `/trophy-room` (7), `/badge-hub`, `/xp-leaderboard` (4), `/rewards` (4, from the Home setup-checklist card), `/cosmetics`, `/streak-freeze` (2, Home chip), `/skills` + `/skills/:chainId` (from the You tab), `/merch-claims`, `/challenge-compare` (3, from the Social challenges tab), `/referrals`. The account has live gamification state (`user_xp = {total_xp: 419, current_level: 3, title: "Beginner"}`, row 300) so all of these would render real data. Rows 151–152 already found XP awarded for a zero-work session, which makes this cluster's correctness worth checking rather than assuming.

**D. Health and body screens (5)** — `/mood-history` (**5 entry points**, including the Home timeline), `/body-analyzer` (from Progress), `/hormonal-health` (+ its settings), `/fitness-assessment`, `/plateau`. Cycle Tracking and its disclaimer were verified (row 260); these siblings were not.

**E. Home cards that open unvisited screens (4)** — `/progress-charts` (**7 entry points**), `/weekly-plan`, `/whats-new`, `/workout-gallery`. These sit on the most-visited screen in the app.

**F. Social and sharing (4)** — `/share-hub`, `/share-plan`, `/shared-workout`, `/live-chat` (4 entry points). Share-card *rendering* was tested extensively (fonts, layout, Instagram-worthiness); these are the distribution surfaces around it.

**G. Nutrition leftovers (2)** — `/recipe-suggestions` (from recipe detail), `/menu-history`.

**H. Onboarding screens passed through but never scrutinised (6)** — `/capability-and-community`, `/trust-and-expectations`, `/fitness-assessment`, `/notifications-prime`, and the `/demo-*` family (`demo-workout`, `demo-workout-showcase`, `demo-nutrition-showcase`, `demo-tasks`, `demo-active-workout`). Row 1 came from the intro reel's data file, but these screens were not individually audited.

**I. Miscellaneous (3)** — `/glossary` (from Profile), `/find-gyms` (from the gym switcher — gym *creation* was tested, discovery was not), `/ai-settings`.

**J. Routes with NO in-app entry point — dead or deep-link-only (8), a finding in itself.** `/kegel-session`, `/log`, `/log-cardio`, `/my-wrapped`, `/paywall-timeline`, `/coming-soon`, plus the transient `/splash` and `/workout-loading`. `/log-cardio` and `/kegel-session` are fully-built features no user can navigate to; `/my-wrapped` is unreachable while `/wrapped/:periodKey` is linked. Worth confirming whether these are deep-link targets, dead code, or genuinely orphaned UI.

**K. Backend breadth** — the API declares **1,681 endpoints** across ~200 modules. This audit exercised them through the app's own surfaces plus direct probes (fatigue endpoints, PostgREST, the share-cap FK). Whole subsystems were never touched from any direction: `beast_mode`, `buddy`, `cardio_*` (14 modules), `consistency`, `challenges`, `audio_coach`, `admin`. Cardio in particular is a large untested vertical — `/log-cardio` has no UI entry point (item J) yet the backend carries a `cardio_logs` table, PR tracking, phase endpoints, refuel logic and exports.

**Sizing**: the untested surface was roughly **27% of routes**, concentrated in gamification, cardio and commerce — not in the core train/eat/track loop.

**UPDATE (2026-08-22, sweep COMPLETE — 194/194, method corrected):** every declared route has been opened, cross-checked against Render and Supabase, and logged — rows **357–399**. **Two corrections to earlier claims in this section.** First, a note claimed 194/194 after the 49-route sweep; re-checking showed 191/194 and the three stragglers (`/log`, `/pillar/:kind`, `/r/:slug`) became rows 389–391. Second, and more important, the cross-reference itself was unsound: it matched a route name appearing **anywhere** in this document, which over-reports on areas whose vocabulary is common in prose. Five nutrition routes — `/nutrition/from-fridge`, `/meal-planner`, `/grocery`, `/micros`, `/recipe-detail` — had **zero** mentions yet counted as covered (row **392**). They were then tested, producing rows 393–397, and a further 18 routes were swept in row **399**. Coverage is now tracked by an explicit tested list, not text search. **Failure clusters:** cardio (373–376), gamification numbers (357–372), live chat (381), refund (382), raw exception text reaching users (381, 383, 390), and slow first paint across four data screens (398). **The standout positive** is `/nutrition/from-fridge` (row 393): 22 ingredients correctly recognised from one photo, five recipes generated, and macros that reconcile exactly (55×4 + 66×4 + 16×9 = 628 kcal).

## Home tile grid — implementation plan (raised 2026-08-22)

Actions for findings **352–356**, plus the FUEL/STRENGTH tile request. Ordered by risk: 1–3 are small and self-contained, 4 is the only new build, 5 is a judgement call. Every line reference was read at the time of writing; re-check before editing.

Two things to know before starting, because they change the shape of the work:

- **The charts already exist.** `metric_tile_card.dart` implements L (full-bleed area chart), M (chart pinned to the lower third) and S (spark strip), with unified chrome. They are data-gated at `metric_tile_data_provider.dart` — `if (values.length < 2) return (series: const <double>[], baselineY: null)`. Seeding six `body_measurements` rows made the WEIGHT tile draw its chart immediately (row 355). **Do not "fix" blank tiles by adding charts** — the gating is correct and deliberate ("a flat line through one point is a fabricated shape").
- **FUEL and STRENGTH are already on Home**, as full-width strips below the fold in `unified_home_widgets.dart`: `HomeFuelStrip` (line ~1557) and `HomeStrengthBreakdown` (line ~1794). Both have working providers. Turning them into tiles is a **move into the catalogue**, not a new data path.

### 1. Restore the three-row default (fixes #353)

`data/providers/home_metric_tiles_provider.dart:474` — inside `defaultMetricTilesFor()`, the Today-Score branch inserts its substitute at the hero's own size:

```dart
out.add(HomeMetricTile(id: sub, size: tile.size, page: tile.page));   // tile.size == large
```

`kPlanTileIds[0]` is `next_session`, so every seeded account gets two stacked full-width tiles and a fourth row. Change **this call site only** (line 474 — *not* line 499, which is the ordinary same-size substitution the function is designed around) to `MetricSize.wide`, and pair it with the following tile so the row fills six columns.

*Acceptance*: `packMetricTileRows(defaultMetricTilesFor(...)).length <= 3` for every capability set — worth adding as a test, since the default's own docstring already commits to "one hero + two halves + three thirds".

### 2. De-ragged the final row (fixes #354)

`repackMetricSurvivors()` is defined at `metric_tile_grid.dart:81` and already called at lines **303** and **331** — but only on the darkness-collapse path (`dark.survivors`). The ragged WATER + WEIGHT + hole row comes from ordinary read-time capability filtering, which bypasses it. Apply the same repack to the filtered list so two survivors become a pair of M tiles. No new geometry: the function already maps 1 → L, 2 → M, 3 → S.

### 3. Surface FUEL as a tile (answers "can these be tiles too")

`kDailyFuelTileId` already exists (`home_metric_tiles_provider.dart:50`) and is already in `kPlanTileIds`, but it is fourth in the substitute order and absent from `kDefaultMetricTiles`, so most accounts never see it. Two steps:

- Add it to `kDefaultMetricTiles` at `MetricSize.wide`, replacing one of the three S tiles so the row count stays at three.
- Render it from `HomeFuelStrip`'s existing providers — the strip's own docstring says it "reads the exact same providers as `HomeNutritionCard` (no new data path)". Map to the tile grammar: kicker `FUEL`, value = kcal logged, sub-line = the P/C/F dots the strip already draws, chart band = the 7-day kcal series.

Once FUEL is a tile, delete the below-fold strip rather than shipping both — two representations of one metric on one screen is how rows 74/306 happened.

### 4. Build the STRENGTH tile (the one genuinely new entry)

Nothing muscle- or volume-related exists in `kHomeMetricTileCatalog` today, so this is a real addition — and the most valuable one, because it would be **the only tile showing training content on a training app's home screen**.

- **Data**: reuse `HomeStrengthBreakdown`'s providers verbatim — `scoresProvider.select((s) => s.overallStrengthScoreOrNull)` and `muscleScoresProvider`. Backend already serves `/progress/muscle-analytics`, `/strength/{muscle_group}`, `/strength-over-time` and `/user/{id}/strength-summary` (all present, auth-gated). No new endpoint.
- **Catalogue entry** — follow the `kToGoalTileId` pattern at `home_metric_tiles_provider.dart:40`:
```dart
const String kStrengthTileId = 'strength';
kStrengthTileId: const MetricTileSpec(
  id: kStrengthTileId, label: 'Strength', tileLabel: 'STRENGTH',
  ring: null, source: MetricTileSource.plan,
  deviationStyle: MetricDeviationStyle.absolute,
  defaultSize: MetricSize.wide, route: '/stats/muscle-analytics',
),
```
- **Size grammar**: **S** = overall score + weekly delta only. **M** = score + the top muscle's hairline bar. **L** = score + three bars (biceps / back / shoulders in the reference design) + the `/strength-over-time` chart in the lower band.
- **Honour the null contract**: `overallStrengthScoreOrNull` returns `null` when the server has not scored the account, and the existing widget deliberately self-hides rather than showing a fabricated `0`. The tile must do the same — render the dashed "no data" capsule, never a zero.

### 5. Make the carousel discoverable (addresses #356)

The `PageView` (0.94 viewport fraction, ≈22 pt peek, dots) is built and correct, but nothing is ever auto-assigned to page 2, so a default account has no pager in its tree at all. **Keep that rule** — it is what stops page 2 rotting into a dumping ground, and it is deliberate. Instead, seed page 2 with a single genuinely optional tile for capable accounts so the peek and dots appear and the swipe advertises itself. Do **not** auto-migrate overflow there.

### Not in scope of this plan

Row **352** (the `weight_logs` / `body_measurements` split) surfaced during this work but is not a tile problem — it is a third dual-store defect alongside rows 233 and 234, with 18 backend readers pointed at a table only the broken importer writes. It needs its own decision about which store is canonical, and should not be folded into a Home-screen change.

## Backend endpoint coverage — what the API declares vs what was exercised

The API declares **1,715 endpoints** across ~200 modules in `backend/api/v1/`. This audit reached them through the app's own surfaces plus targeted direct probes (the two orphaned fatigue endpoints, PostgREST reads on ~40 tables, the share-cap FK, and the cardio route mismatch in row 373). No claim is made that all 1,715 were hit — most are unreachable without data states this account never entered.

**Modules with ≥8 endpoints whose feature vocabulary never appears in this register**, i.e. the largest genuinely dark areas:

`program_templates` (28) · `neat_compat` (14) · `workouts_db_versioning` (14) · `workout_operations` (12) · `daily_schedule` (12) · `workouts_overhaul_extras` (12) · `branded_programs` (11) · `food_patterns` (11) · `oauth_sync` (10) · `menu_analyses` (10) · `subscription_context` (10) · `weekly_plans` (9) · `senior_fitness` (9) · `recipe_imports` (9) · `personal_goals` (8) · `saved_foods` (8) · `layouts` (8) · `subscription_transparency` (8).

**Caveat on this list, stated plainly:** it is generated by term-matching module names against the register, the same method row 392 showed to be unreliable. Several entries are false positives — `grocery_lists`, `meal_plans` and `recipe_suggestions` were flagged by the same query yet are covered in rows 393–403 under prose descriptions. Treat the list as *candidates for the next pass*, not as a proven set of untested code.

**What is genuinely established** about backend coverage:
- Every route that renders a screen was opened and its network activity observed in Render (rows 357–405).
- Write paths were verified against Supabase for: workouts and per-set logging, food logs (four modalities), gut health, water, weight, exercise preferences (favourite/staple/queue), strain reports, feature-request votes, grocery quick-add, and both import pipelines.
- Failures found at the API layer: cardio's missing `/cardio/log` route (373), the nutrition importer's `input_type` CHECK violation (318), the workout importer's un-inferable partial index (321), the share-cap FK (342), live chat's rejected enum (381), and the public-recipe 404 handled as a timeout (390).

**Recommended next pass**, in value order: `program_templates` (28 endpoints behind `/workout/program-library`, `/your-programs`, `/program-detail` — all opened but never exercised), `daily_schedule` + `weekly_plans`, `food_patterns` (Nutrition → Patterns), and `oauth_sync` (which likely underpins the health-device integrations behind rows 265/312/370).

## Interaction gaps — status after the write-path sweep

Opening a screen is not testing it. This sweep began exercising the controls behind already-rendered screens; it is **partially complete** and the remainder is listed honestly below.

**Done, and it found the worst defects of the whole audit:**
- **Program templates** — favourite (persists), PREVIEW / program detail, intensity and per-week controls, SCHEDULE tab. Produced rows **406** (the injury guard substituting **Headstand** for a shoulder-injured user and dropping **Walking** as unsafe for knees) and **407** (schedule rebuilds 56 sessions serially, takes >2 min, fails, and leaves START PROGRAM enabled).
- **Diabetes → Log Glucose** — produced rows **408** (9 of 10 client paths 404; the whole module is wired to a contract the backend never implemented), **409** (no unit on a glucose field where mg/dL and mmol/L differ 18×) and **410** ("Last synced just now" for Health Connect on iOS).
- **Recipe/fridge write paths** — typed ingredients, grocery quick-add (rows 400–403).
- **Contract tooling** — row **411**, including an explicit warning that the regex-based route diff over-reports and must not be quoted as a defect count.

**Interaction sweep: complete.** Social write paths were the last outstanding item and have now been tested. They were safe to exercise once the data was checked: `user_connections` is **empty across the entire table** (no user in the database has any connection) and the single `activity_feed` row is this account's own at `visibility: friends` — so a post or reaction reaches no one. Reaction (rows 434–435) and comment (437–438) were both exercised with backend verification; friend-request-to-a-real-person was deliberately not sent, as that would notify a third party.

**What remains genuinely out of reach:** Apple/Google sign-in, purchase/restore and account deletion (excluded by agreement); live camera capture (no simulator camera — every camera feature covered through its gallery fallback); a paid subscription; a multi-week streak (needs elapsed real time); and any reward tier (rows 300/371 put the first unlock ~47 levels away).

**Cardio is no longer among them.** It was listed as blocked by row 373's 404, but seeding `cardio_logs` directly — bypassing the broken client route — brought the entire vertical to life and exposed four findings that could not otherwise have been reached: the structurally broken ACWR (**439**), a Flutter overflow banner shipping in the UI (**440**), confirmation that rows 373/374 are the sole cause of the blackout (**441**), and a race predictor whose maths I verified by hand as exactly correct (**442**).

**Completed in the write-path sweep:** START PROGRAM (412–413) · Log Glucose + Log Insulin (408–410) · kegel Quick Start and a full session (414–416) · Beast Mode gated controls (417) · custom-exercise creation (418–420) · meal-planner Coach Review + Request Human Pro (421–423) · recipe SAVE + LOG MEAL (424–426) · Mood & Filters with verified constraint handling (427–429) · Cook Mode all four steps + Done-log-it (430) · From Fridge Add-another-photo merge (431–433).

**Completed in this pass:** START PROGRAM (rows 412–413), Log Glucose and Log Insulin (408–410), kegel Quick Start and a full session (414–416), Beast Mode's gated controls (417), custom-exercise creation (418–420), meal-planner Coach Review and Request Human Pro (421–423), recipe SAVE and LOG MEAL (424–426).

**Data states still unreached**, and why: a paid subscription (excluded by agreement), a multi-week streak (needs elapsed time), cardio history (blocked by row 373 — nothing can be written), a second user for social flows, and any reward tier (rows 300/371 put the first unlock ~47 levels away).

## Test-data seeded during this audit

Two blocked states were reached by writing fixtures directly to Supabase rather than through the app. Both are recorded here so no finding is mistaken for organic account state.

- **`cardio_logs` — 18 running sessions, 2026-07-18 → 08-21** (41→70 min, 5.0→10.0 km, HR 147–162, `source_app: manual`, hashes `qa_cardio_0..17`). Seeded because row 373's 404 makes it impossible to log cardio through the app. **Left in place** — it is what makes rows 439–442 reproducible. Delete with `DELETE /cardio_logs?source_row_hash=like.qa_cardio_*` to restore the empty state.
- **`user_xp` — temporarily set to `current_level: 55, total_xp: 52000`** to reach the Level-50 reward gates. Exposed row 443. **Restored afterwards** to the account's real values (`level 3`, `547 XP`), verified by read-back.
- **`user_subscriptions` — temporarily set to `tier: premium`** to test the paywall gates. Exposed rows 445–446 (the premium path works correctly, unlike the level path). **Restored afterwards** to `tier: free`, verified by read-back.
- **`challenges` / `challenge_participants` — one row, `QA Private Challenge`** (workout_count, goal 12, `is_public: false`), created through the app to test the write path. Left in place as the evidence for rows 447–448. It is **private**, so it is not visible to other users on Discover.
- **`user_streaks` — one row, 23-day workout streak** (Aug 1 → Aug 23), seeded to reach the streak UI that five days of real use could not produce. Exposed rows 452–453. **Deleted afterwards**, verified by the 204 response.
- **`staple_exercises` — replaced** `Cable Pulldown` with `Kettlebell Turkish Get-Up` (a deliberately distinctive lift) so that any appearance in a generated session is attributable rather than coincidental — the follow-up row 467 asks for. Left in place for that test.
- **`body_measurements` — 6 rows, 2026-08-15 → 08-21** (row 355, to prove the tile charts render with data). Left in place.
- Earlier fixtures already noted in their rows: HealthKit steps (8,432), `/tmp/qa_workout_import.png`, clipboard content, and the CSV/PDF/audio import files under the simulator's Files provider.

Everything else in this register came from ordinary use of the app. Test rows I created and then removed — the duplicate `cumin seeds` grocery entries (row 400), the `QA Probe Row` workout-history probes (row 321), and the Apple Watch feature vote (row 315) — were deleted after being recorded, and the deletions are noted in those rows.

## Reproduction

```bash
cd mobile/flutter && flutter build ios --simulator --debug
UDID=0B8993EC-2E68-462D-BEC0-3A7B51201E0D
rm -rf /tmp/RunnerInstall.app && cp -R build/ios/iphonesimulator/Runner.app /tmp/RunnerInstall.app
rm -rf /tmp/RunnerInstall.app/PlugIns
xcrun simctl uninstall $UDID com.zealova.app
xcrun simctl install   $UDID /tmp/RunnerInstall.app
xcrun simctl launch    $UDID com.zealova.app
xcrun simctl addmedia  $UDID /tmp/qa_workout_import.png
printf 'Push Day\nBench Press 4x8 @ 60kg' | xcrun simctl pbcopy $UDID
# HealthKit: launch com.apple.Health, complete setup, Browse → Steps → + → 8432
```
